### Task 8: the direct-mode server — the request loop, the timers, the fold, the continuation

**Files:**
- Create: `ccd/codexbridge/stream.py`, `ccd/codexbridge/server.py`
- Modify: `server/test/codexBridgeFixture.ts` (`plantBridgeLane`, `spawnBridge`),
  `server/test/fixtures/codexbridge/driver.py` (the `serve` op)
- Test: `server/test/codex-bridge-server.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 1-7 (the reducer is built with the request's `name_map` and `tools_required`); Stage 1's `read`,
  `ensure_fresh`, `claims_of`, `held`, `AuthError` and `Reading.account_id`; Task 6's three functions.
- Produces:
  - `stream.AnthropicStream(write: Callable[[bytes], None], *, model: str, message_id: str)`: `commit(status: int =
    200) -> None` (headers, then `message_start` with zero usage and the requested model); `blocks(events) -> None`
    (renumbers local indexes across continuations); `ping() -> None`; `finish(stop_reason: str, usage: dict) ->
    None`; `fail(e: BridgeError) -> None` (closes an open block, then the `error` frame); `last_write_at: float`.
  - `stream.fold(events) -> dict` — the non-streaming message `{id, type: "message", role: "assistant", model,
    content, stop_reason, stop_sequence: null, usage}`.
  - `server.BridgeConfig(NamedTuple)` and `config_from_env(environ: Mapping[str, str]) -> BridgeConfig` (the
    contract's environment; a missing or malformed value exits with one `ccgpt-proxy: direct: <reason>` line).
  - `server.Hooks(NamedTuple)`: `read_body`, `content_encoding`, `decode_body`, `apply_effort`, `server_class` (the
    shim's `_read_request_body`, `_content_encoding`, `_decode_body` and `_Server`, passed in by Task 9 so none is
    copied; an encoding the shim does not implement is its 415, as today). `apply_effort(data: dict) ->
    Tuple[dict, object]` returns the body and the `output_config` value the shim's `_apply_effort` popped: Task 9
    passes a two-line wrapper that reads `data.get("output_config")` and then calls `_apply_effort(data)`, so
    `_apply_effort` itself is unchanged and `litellm` mode stays byte for byte.
  - `server.serve(cfg: BridgeConfig, hooks: Hooks) -> int`.
  - `server.load_auth_lib(path: str) -> module` — the one load of Stage 1's library (`spec_from_file_location`),
    cached for the process; every library call the bridge makes goes through the module it returned. A load that
    fails raises, and the request path answers it as 401 `auth-library-absent` (the contract's error table).
  - **`driver.py`'s `serve` op** (`{"op": "serve"}`, the last op of its action; it prints no op line and exits with
    `serve`'s return). In order: it loads `ccd/ccgpt-proxy.py` by path, unchanged (`spec_from_file_location` under a
    module name other than `__main__`, so its `__main__` block, `ccd/ccgpt-proxy.py:1554`, does not run; the
    contract's environment carries the three variables its top level reads, `:962-964`), and takes the five hooks
    from that module, copying none: `Hooks(m._read_request_body, m._content_encoding, m._decode_body, <apply_effort>,
    m._Server)`, where `<apply_effort>` is the op's own two-line wrapper, the one stated copy (Task 9's lives inside
    `_run_direct`, and Task 9 is built after this task): `oc = data.get("output_config"); return
    m._apply_effort(data), oc`, which reads `output_config` before the call that pops it. Only when `consts` carries
    a `ccgpt_auth.<NAME>` key, it calls `server.load_auth_lib(os.environ["CCRC_CODEX_AUTH_LIB"])` and sets each such
    const on the module that call returned; otherwise the bridge loads the library at its first request, so an absent
    library is the bridge's own 401 `auth-library-absent`, not a driver crash. It sets each `<module>.<NAME>` const on
    the package's module, as before, then calls `server.serve(config_from_env(os.environ), hooks)`.
  - `spawnBridge(home: string, lane: string, o?: {env?: Record<string, string>; consts?: Record<string, unknown>;
    timeoutMs?: number}): Promise<{port: number; key: string; proc: ChildProcess; stderr(): string; stop(signal?:
    NodeJS.Signals): Promise<{code: number | null; signal: string | null}>}>` — runs `python3 -I driver.py` with
    `{"pkg": <ccd dir>, "ops": [{"op": "serve"}], "consts": o.consts}` and the contract's environment for the lane
    `plantBridgeLane` planted under `home` (`o.env` names the test upstream and auth bases, else `CLOSED_AUTH_BASE`),
    and resolves once `GET /ccgpt/lane` answers on `port`; `key` is the lane's key, read from its `runtime.env`.
  - **How a test constant reaches the library** (`LOCK_WAIT_S`, for example). `spawnBridge` runs the bridge through
    the `serve` op, so the driver's `consts` apply to it. A key `ccgpt_auth.<NAME>` names the library: the op sets it
    on the module `load_auth_lib` returned, as Stage 1's driver sets each `consts` entry on the module it loaded
    (Stage 1 plan, Task 1, the `driver.py` bullet), and then serves, so the bridge's later calls, through the same
    cached load, reach the same module. The override takes effect because Stage 1's library binds no constant early
    (the same bullet: every bound argument defaults to `None` and resolves to its module constant when the function
    is called).
  - Constants, read when called: `PING_S = 15.0`, `HOLD_S = 15.0`, `DRAIN_S = 5.0`, `STOP_MARGIN_S = 5.0`,
    `CONTINUE_MAX = 3`, `REFRESH_TICK_S = 60.0`, `FRESH_TOKEN_S = 300.0`. `DRAIN_S + STOP_MARGIN_S` is at most the
    nohup stop's TERM-to-KILL wait (Task 11), pinned there.
- **One `/v1/messages` request**, in order: key check; read the body through `hooks.read_body`; `hooks.apply_effort`
  (one effort resolution, the shim's; it hands back the popped `output_config`); a `model` outside the catalogue → 404
  (the contract's Routes; the catalogue file is read at each check, never once for the process, so a refresh that adds a
  model and a class move to it reach a running bridge, Task 11b); `inbound.from_anthropic(…, output_config=<popped>)`
  (its `BridgeError` → the HTTP answer); the token through `ensure_fresh(auth_dir, lane=lane)` (an `AuthError` →
  `errors.from_auth`: 401 for a dead word, 503 for `refresh-failed`/`auth-lock-busy`); `backend.post` with `account_id =
  reading.account_id`; a 401 → `refresh_rejected` with that token and one retry, EXCEPT when the rejected token is
  itself fresh (its `iat` claim, through `claims_of`, within `FRESH_TOKEN_S` of now): then no refresh, 401 at once with
  the upstream code recorded, counted `refresh-skipped-fresh-token`, so a 401 a refresh cannot cure (an `originator`
  refusal, D27) never spends a refresh-token rotation per turn; a non-2xx → `classify_http` → the HTTP answer and
  `record_upstream`; a 2xx → start the `HOLD_S` clock, feed the reducer, release blocks, commit at the commit point,
  ping on downstream silence, stop reading the moment `Reducer.terminal` is set (never waiting for EOF after
  `response.failed`), and end with `api_error` when `IDLE_TIMEOUT_S` passes with no PARSED event (keepalive comments do
  not reset it); a continuation when `needs_continuation`; `finish` with the stop reason and the usage; then
  `store.remember`, `stats.usage(…, image_count=req.image_count, continued=<the message was continued>)`,
  `stats.outcome` (`2xx`, `compactionRequests` when `req.compaction`, `continuations`, and the reducer's `counts`),
  `record_upstream(state="ok")` only when the status file read `limit-paused` or `ineligible`. On
  `context_length_exceeded`, at either point, the answer is `overflow(N, M)` with `N = estimate(countable_bytes, ratio,
  image_count)` and `M = min(window, floor(N × 0.9 / 1000) × 1000)`, where `window` is the lane's
  `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS` read from `<configDir>/settings.json` at that moment, 200000 when absent; so `N > M`
  always, and `stats.refusal("backend", model, N, window)` records it (D-6415).
- `stream:false` runs the same pipeline into `fold` and answers one JSON message, or the HTTP error (counted
  `streamFalse`). `count_tokens` runs `inbound.from_anthropic` (with the store's `lookup`) and answers
  `{"input_tokens": estimate(countable_bytes, ratio, image_count)}` locally, never upstream, never 501.
  `/v1/models` answers the lane catalogue's model ids in Anthropic's list shape. A `refresher` thread calls
  `ensure_fresh(auth_dir, within_s=half_life_s(r), lane=lane)` every `REFRESH_TICK_S`, so an idle lane's token is
  refreshed (the designated refresher, spec §2.6).
- **Every handler is wrapped**: an exception anywhere in a request answers `api_error` (a 503 before the commit
  point, an `error` event after it) and logs one stderr line, `ccgpt-proxy: direct: <class name> <code>` with the
  code bounded by `CODE_RE`, never the exception's text, an argument or a traceback.
- **The drain** (SIGTERM; D-6429). Open streams are kept in a lock-guarded registry with an in-flight
  counter. The signal handler only sets `stopping` and starts a thread that calls `httpd.shutdown()` (a
  `ThreadingHTTPServer`'s `serve_forever` cannot be shut down from its own thread, and its handler threads are
  daemon threads, so leaving them to the interpreter's exit would drop them). When `serve_forever` returns on the
  main thread: `httpd.server_close()` (new connections are refused from here, which is up to one poll interval
  after the signal: `serve_forever` notices the shutdown at its next poll, every 0.5 s by default, and until
  `server_close` the listening socket keeps accepting into its backlog), wait on the counter up to `DRAIN_S`,
  then `fail(api_error)` every stream still open (counted `drain-cut`), wait up to 1 s for those writes, flush the
  stats file, return 0. Claude Code re-sends a cut stream as `stream:false` at once (`stub.md` runs 06/07), and that
  re-send is served only if the next process listens within Claude Code's retry window (Review Focus 3).

  `CCGPT_BRIDGE_DIR`'s parent at `sys.path[0]` (with Task 18's two CLIs, the only `sys.path` edits in shipped code;
  `-I` keeps every other path off it), imports `codexbridge`, calls `codexbridge.load_all()` and
  `sys.exit(codexbridge.server.serve(config_from_env(os.environ), Hooks(_read_request_body, _content_encoding,
  _decode_body, _effort_and_output_config, _Server)))`, where `_effort_and_output_config(data)` is
  `oc = data.get("output_config"); return _apply_effort(data), oc`, defined inside `_run_direct`. Any other value of
  `CCGPT_BRIDGE` exits 2 with `ccgpt-proxy: unknown CCGPT_BRIDGE '<v>'`; unset runs today's lines unchanged.

---

# Wave 2 — the lane (one PR)

**What it delivers:** the two roster fields, the `bridge` tier in `ccrc codex start|stop|status`, `ccrc codex
switch`, the hourly auto-advance on a `direct` lane, the doctor's mode-aware rows, the poller's direct arm, D25, and
the docs.
**PR boundary:** cut after wave 1 and Stage 1 wave 2 merged; ends when Task 16 is green.
**Effect on a live box:** the install spine re-renders, once, the files of each codex lane whose `lane.json` reads stale
(every `lane.json` from before this wave gains `bridge`, `upstream` and `bridgeFiles`, Task 11's `_inst_codex_lanes`; a
lane with no `lane.json` is left for its first start, D-6872), under the lane-files lock and before the doctor runs, so
the update's doctor stays clean (without it every lane's `lane.json` would read stale, the doctor would FAIL each one,
and `ccrc update` would exit 3). The re-render also rewrites each lane's settings env block, TSV and effort file from
unchanged inputs. Nothing else changes until an operator runs `ccrc codex switch <id> direct`: every lane's
`exec.bridge` is absent, which reads `litellm`. A `litellm` lane's usage rows are today's, word for word; two `lane.json` FAIL sentences, the `stale` one and the class-registry one, are reworded (the whole-wave fix round, Y7); and the bridge files' 0600 row is asked in either mode (D-6887).

  `server/test/codexLaneFixture.ts` (its planted `lane.json` gains the three keys); `README.md` (the mirror table,

  (`shared/roster.ts`, mirrored once in `roster-json.mjs` and pinned equal by `roster-exec-parity.test.ts`); the
  codex arm gains `bridge: CodexBridgeMode` and `upstream: CodexUpstream`, defaulted when absent (`litellm`,
  `backend`, the first entry of each set), and a present value outside the set REFUSED by both validators

  - Node: `rosterFromJson`'s codex return (`shared/roster-json.mjs:523-535`) carries the RESOLVED `bridge` and
    `upstream` beside `proxyPort`, `litellmPort` and `authDir`; `readRoster` keeps that parsed result (today it

    stdout, rc 0; rc 1 with `lane-mode-unreadable` when `.bridge` is absent or not one of the two words, never a
    default. Its twin `_codex_lane_upstream <id>` → `backend` | `siwc`, rc 1 `lane-upstream-unreadable` on the same
    terms, reads `.upstream`; it is the one bash reader behind every `upstream-unsupported` refusal (Tasks 11, 12)
    and the doctor's `siwc` FAIL (Task 13). No second `jq` reads the roster's `exec.bridge` or `exec.upstream`;
    `_codex_row` is unchanged. Every caller captures the mode and its rc first (`mode="$(_codex_lane_mode "$id")";
    mrc=$?`), never inside another command's argument: on rc 1, install prints one `NOT measured` line for the lane
    and skips its tiers (counted as the lock-busy lane is), and start and the doctor refuse. Stop, account removal
    and `_uninst_codex` stay mode-blind on rc 1: they walk `bridge shim litellm` in that fixed order, stop only what
    `_codex_tier_ours` proves, and never skip the lane (Task 11).
  `lane.json` gains `bridge`, `upstream` and `bridgeFiles: {stats: "bridge.stats.json", store: "reasoning.sqlite",
  recordings: "recordings"}`. `_codex_lane_json_state` checks the three for PRESENCE (strings, and an object of three
  strings), as it checks `units` today and for the same reason (`ccd/ccrc:12124-12126`), so a `lane.json` from before
  this plan reads `stale` and is re-rendered; whether the value matches the roster is `materialise --check`'s
  question (the doctor's), the one comparison. A hand edit of `exec.bridge` is therefore not a switch: the next
  materialise renders it, and `start` then refuses `retired-tier-running` until `ccrc codex switch` runs (Task 11).

### Task 11: the `bridge` tier

**Files:**
- Modify: `ccd/ccrc` — `CODEX_TIERS` (`:11943`), `_codex_tier_port` (`:11989-11998`), `_codex_lane_answer`
  (`:12286-12288`), `_codex_unit` (`:12294-12299`), `_codex_pid_is_tier` (`:12311-12320`), `_codex_tier_ours`
  (`:12491-12585`, the shim arm `:12540-12561`), `_codex_started_json` (`:12615-12635`), `_codex_tier_stale`,
  `_codex_start_tier` (`:12965-13020`), `_codex_stop_tier`'s nohup wait (`:13093-13108`), `_codex_stop_lane`
  (`:13146-13154`), `_codex_cmd_start` (`:13173-13305`; its per-tier loops `:13207-13228` become the lane's mode tiers),
  `_codex_cmd_status` (`:13335-13462`), `_codex_reap_files` (`:12668-12700`), a new spine step `_inst_codex_lanes` right
  before `_inst_codex_tiers` (`:13614`), `_inst_codex_tiers` (`:16737+`, its loop `:16779`), `_uninst_codex`
  (`:24805-24922`, its tier list `:24812`) and `_uninst_codex_by_hand` (`:24800`), the usage text (`:2436-2449`)
- Test: `server/test/ccrc-codex.test.ts`, `server/test/codexLaneFixture.ts`, `server/test/ccrc-account.test.ts`,
  `server/test/ccrc-install.test.ts`, `server/test/ccrc-uninstall.test.ts`, and any suite that pins the spine's step
  order (`git grep -n _inst_codex_tiers server/test` lists them, `ccrc-doctor.test.ts:13219` among them)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - `CODEX_TIERS="litellm shim bridge"`; `_codex_mode_tiers <mode>` → `litellm shim` | `bridge`, and rc 2 with
    nothing printed for any other word, an empty one included, never a default; `_codex_other_tiers <mode>` → the
    other mode's, on the same terms; `_codex_bridge_dir` → `$CCRC_HERE/codexbridge`;
    `_codex_bridge_digest` → sha256 over the sorted `<name> <sha256>` lines of the package's `*.py`; `_codex_python`
    → the system `python3`, refused `python-too-old` below 3.9. Every loop over the tiers that a mode decides
    (`_codex_cmd_start`'s two, `_inst_codex_tiers`', the doctor's, and the advance step's readiness, Task 11b) walks
    `_codex_mode_tiers "$mode"` with the mode captured first (Task 10's rule for rc 1), never `$CODEX_TIERS`; only
    the reap's file names and the mode-blind stop walk all three.
  - `_codex_tier_port bridge` → `CX_PROXY` (the shim's port, D19); `_codex_unit <id> bridge` → `lane.json`'s
    `units.shim` (the bridge runs under the shim's unit name, so the two can never be active at once).
  - **Identity, per tier** (`_codex_tier_ours`). `_codex_lane_answer <port>` prints `<shape> <lane>` on one line:
    `shim` for exactly `keys == ["lane"]` (today's predicate), `direct` for exactly `{"lane": <string>, "mode":
    "direct"}`, rc 2 for anything else. It prints the shape rather than setting a variable because its one caller
    runs it in a command substitution (`lane="$(_codex_lane_answer "$port")"`, `:12541`), a subshell whose CX_*
    never reach the caller (`:12152-12153`); `_codex_tier_ours` splits the line with `read -r shape lane`. The
    **shim** is the `shim`-shaped answer for this lane (as today, a proven handle not required); a `direct`-shaped
    answer on the shim tier is rc 2 with `CX_TIER_WHY=listener-other-mode`. The **bridge** is the `direct`-shaped
    answer for this lane AND a pid proven by its argv (`_codex_pid_is_tier <id> bridge`) that itself holds the port
    (`_codex_pid_listens`); a `direct` answer with no proven pid is rc 2 `listener-unidentified`, a `shim`-shaped
    answer on the bridge tier is rc 2 `listener-other-mode`, a proven pid with the port not answering yet is 4.
  - **The other mode's tier on a healthy lane.** The two modes share one unit (`units.shim`) and one port, so on
    every healthy lane the other mode's tier reads rc 2: `listener-other-mode` while the lane's own mode tier
    answers, or `unit-unproven` (`:12583`) while the shared port is silent and the shared unit's MainPID is proven,
    by `_codex_pid_is_tier`, as the lane's own mode tier (a start in progress). The rule: while the lane's own mode
    tier reads 0 or 4, the other mode's tier reading either of those is `not running` in status, start, stop,
    `_inst_codex_tiers`, `_uninst_codex` and the doctor, and none of them calls `_codex_foreign_what` for it, whose
    unit arm ("A live unit of this tier's name, whatever else answers", `:12852`) would name the shared unit
    foreign. One predicate reads it, `_codex_tier_is_other_mode <id> <tier> <rc>`, called directly after that
    `_codex_tier_ours` call as `_codex_tier_is_our_handle` is (`:12587-12600`), with the own mode tier measured first:
    each walk puts the lane's own mode tiers first.
  - The bridge's argv is `<python3> -I <shim file> --ccrc-bridge=direct --ccrc-lane=<id>`, the `--ccrc-bridge` word
    BEFORE `--ccrc-lane`. `_codex_pid_is_tier <id> bridge` wants `<shim file> --ccrc-bridge=direct --ccrc-lane=<id>`,
    without the interpreter (as the litellm `want` leaves it out, `:12315`; `ps` may print `/usr/bin/python3`). A
    build from before this plan wants `<shim file> --ccrc-lane=<id>` (`:12316`), which that argv does not contain, so
    it never proves the bridge's pid as its shim's either. Its record is `bridge.started` = `{"interpreter", "code"
    (the sha256 of the shim, then the auth library, D-6874), "bridge" (the digest), "mode": "direct"}`, its pidfile
    `bridge.pid`; `interpreter` is recorded but not compared (D-6875).
  - `_codex_start_tier <id> bridge` spawns with the contract's environment and, as pairs, `CCGPT_BRIDGE_UPSTREAM_BASE`
    and `CCRC_CODEX_AUTH_BASE` each ONLY when the caller has it set (a tier's environment is never the caller's,
    `:1218-1222`); both readers refuse a non-loopback value. It refuses `auth-library-absent` (remedy `ccrc update`)
    when `CCRC_CODEX_AUTH_LIB`'s file is not a regular readable file, as Stage 1's litellm arm does.
  - **No runtime gate for the bridge.** Today both `_codex_started_json` and `_codex_start_tier` read the LiteLLM
    runtime before any per-tier choice: the first returns 1 when `ccgpt-runtime python` answers nothing
    (`:12617-12619`), which `_codex_tier_stale` reads as 2, "cannot tell", and the second refuses `runtime-absent`
    for every tier (`:12976-12979`). In both, the `bridge` arm is decided BEFORE that runtime read, and its record's
    `interpreter` is `_codex_python`.
  - `ccrc codex start <id>` on a `direct` lane: no `litellm.yaml`, no runtime required, the bridge tier only;
    refuses `retired-tier-running` when the litellm tier reads this lane's (`0` or `4`), or the shim tier does (a
    `shim`-shaped answer for this lane, which the bridge tier reads as `listener-other-mode`: a shim on the shared
    port), never adopting and never stopping either (that is `switch`'s); refuses `upstream-unsupported` for
    `exec.upstream: siwc` ("arrives in Stage 3"). On a `litellm` lane a running bridge (the bridge tier `0`/`4`,
    which the shim tier reads as `listener-other-mode`) is `retired-tier-running` too. Every other mode's rc 2 is
    read by the rule above. The rekey branch restarts the bridge tier on a `direct` lane.
  - `ccrc codex stop <id>` and account removal stop all three tiers, mode-blind, the lane's own mode's tiers front
    first and then the other mode's (`direct`: bridge, shim, litellm; `litellm`: shim, litellm, bridge), so each tier
    is measured after the one holding the shared port has gone (`not running` for the absent ones); `_codex_stop_lane`
    prints `litellm <w>, shim <w>, bridge <w>`. When `_codex_lane_mode` answers rc 1 (Task 10), both walk `bridge shim
    litellm` in that fixed order and stop only what `_codex_tier_ours` proves; neither skips the lane.
  - **`ccrc uninstall` stops a bridge too.** `_uninst_codex` keeps its own hard-coded `local -a tiers=(shim
    litellm)` (`:24812`) and stops a tier only when `_codex_tier_ours` proves it (`:24847-24868`), so on a `direct`
    lane it would leave a running bridge, the credential's refresher, behind ccrc. Its tier list becomes
    `_codex_stop_lane`'s order for the lane's mode (`direct`: bridge, shim, litellm), read through the same rule for
    the other mode's tier. On rc 1 from `_codex_lane_mode` it stays mode-blind, like the stop: it walks `bridge shim
    litellm` in that fixed order, stops only what `_codex_tier_ours` proves, and never skips the lane, so a `lane.json`
    from before this plan, which has no `bridge` key, still has its tiers stopped (today's `(shim litellm)` stops them,
    `:24812`, `:24847-24868`). `_uninst_codex_by_hand <id> bridge` names the unit the bridge runs under (the shim's,
    `ccgpt-<id>-shim.service`, `deploy/models-op.mjs:896`), its pidfile `bridge.pid` and its argv marker
    `--ccrc-bridge=direct --ccrc-lane=<id>`.
  - **The stop waits for the drain.** The nohup arm's TERM-to-KILL wait (`:13096`, ten 1 s polls) is
    `_codex_stop_wait_s <tier>`: 10 for every tier, which is at least the bridge's `DRAIN_S + STOP_MARGIN_S` (5 + 5).
    The systemd arm is `systemctl --user stop` (`:1001`), which waits the unit's `TimeoutStopSec`; `systemd-run` sets
    none (`:1284-1287`), so it is the manager's `DefaultTimeoutStopSec`, 90 s by systemd's default. There is no
    launchd arm for a tier: `_svc_run_supervised` has the systemd and nohup arms only (`:1284-1302`), so a macOS box
    runs the nohup arm.
  - `ccrc codex status <id> --json` gains `mode`, `upstream`, `tiers.bridge`, and `bridge: {statsAt,
    lastUpstreamRequestId, refusals, outcomes, unexplained}` read from the stats file (`null` when absent);
    `tiers.litellm` and `tiers.shim` keep their shape.
  - `_codex_reap_files` removes `bridge.started`, `bridge.pid` (by its `CODEX_TIERS` loop, `:12679`), the four
    markers (`bridge-instructions`, `record-streams`, `liveprobe-allowed`, `liveprobe.count`), the stats file and the
    store with its `-wal`/`-shm` (names read from `lane.json`'s `bridgeFiles` before `lane.json` itself goes); and the
    recordings directory by a bounded recursive removal: only when `bridgeFiles.recordings` matches
    `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$` and `$dir/<name>` is a real directory (not a symlink) directly under the lane directory.
  - `_inst_codex_lanes` (new spine step, before `_inst_codex_tiers`; skipped on a `server`-role box like it): for each
    codex lane, `_codex_lane_json_ensure <id>`, which re-renders through `_models_node_locked … materialise` (the
    lane-files lock) when `lane.json` reads `stale`; a failure is one line and one `codex-lanes` in
    `INST_DEGRADED`, never a stop.
  - `_inst_codex_tiers` walks the lane's mode tiers; a running stale bridge is restarted (stop, then start: a stream
    longer than `DRAIN_S` is cut with `api_error`, and requests in the gap can fail, Review Focus 3).

### Task 11b: the hourly auto-advance on a `direct` lane

Two gates of today's advance step would end it, silently, on every `direct` lane. (a) Its readiness question is a
hard-coded `for tier in litellm shim` that returns at once unless each answers rc 0 (`ccd/ccrc:11652-11657`), then
the check-only `litellm` comparison (`_models_advance_check`, `:11550-11556`, reading `_models_adv_body_check`,
`:11458-11463`); on a `direct` lane the litellm tier reads 1 and the shim tier reads 2. (b) The canary's lane probe
accepts only an answer whose one key is `lane` (`deploy/models-canary.mjs:160-170`) and sends its keyed request only
when that answer names the lane (`:223`), so the bridge's `{"lane": <id>, "mode": "direct"}` makes every canary
`unavailable`; `_models_canary` (`ccd/ccrc:11329`) has one caller, the advance step's canary read (`:11472`).

**What the bridge reads for a model id**, from this plan's contract: the requested `model` passes through upstream
unchanged after one check against the lane catalogue (`CCGPT_CATALOGUE`, the contract's Routes; read at each check,
Task 8). It reads neither the class registry nor the settings env block for a model id; its one `settings.json`
read is the window, at an overflow (Task 8). A class move (`advance-commit`) renames the registry and materialises,
which re-renders the env block (`deploy/models-op.mjs:1545-1548`, "THE MOVE"), and takes its target from the
catalogue (`candidateFor(registry, catalogue, …)`, `:1634`). So on a `direct` lane a move is the registry and the
env re-render, with no tier restart.

**What the refresh does before the step on a `direct` lane.** The step runs only for a lane whose refresh row came back
ok (`adv_ids`, `ccd/ccrc:11826`), and two parts of that first pass are not the bridge's. (1) The catalogue probe:
`_models_probe_codex_env` hands it the LiteLLM runtime's interpreter (`CCRC_CODEX_PYTHON` from `ccgpt-runtime python`,
`:10490-10492`), and `_fetch_codex_lane` refuses `runtime-absent` without one (`ccd/ccrc-models-probe:179-182`). In
Stage 2 a `direct` lane's catalogue probe still runs on that interpreter, unchanged: the runtime stays installed until
Stage 4 retires LiteLLM, so a `direct` lane's auto-advance needs it, while its tiers do not (Task 11). (2) The render:
the refresh runs `_models_litellm` for every codex-probe lane (`:11797-11799`). It keeps doing so on a `direct` lane, so
`litellm.yaml` stays current and a switch back to `litellm` (Task 12) finds it so. Measured at the render's restart
step, `_models_litellm_lane_held` (`:10946-11059`): it returns before asking about any tier when the bytes are unchanged
(`:10955-10958`); it stops a tier only when `_codex_tier_ours <id> litellm` reads 0 or 4 (`held=1`, `:10980`), a 1 does
nothing (`:11027`), and both starts run only when `held` is 1 (`:11037`, `:11052`). So it already acts only on a running
LiteLLM tier, and on a `direct` lane that tier reads 1 unless a retired one is running, which start refuses as
`retired-tier-running` (Task 11). This task makes it so for that case too: the function captures `_codex_lane_mode <id>`
before its tier question (`:10959`; Task 10's rule), and on `direct` it asks no tier, leaves `held` at 0 and writes
only. On `litellm`, or rc 1, it is today's function. On a `direct` lane the render therefore never stops, restarts or
starts a tier.

**Files:**
- Modify: `ccd/ccrc` (`_models_advance_steps`' readiness, `:11649-11658`; `_models_litellm_lane_held`, `:10946-11059`);
  `deploy/models-canary.mjs` (`laneInputs` `:123-141`, `laneOf` `:160-170`)
- Test: `server/test/ccrc-models-advance.test.ts`, `server/test/models-canary.test.ts` (its planter, `:33`, and the
  base objects at `:74-75` write `bridge: "litellm"`, and the existing passing cases stay green with it),
  `server/test/codexLaneFixture.ts` (`laneProxyAnswersAs`, `:2354-2356`, gains a direct-shape answer mode: the lane
  plus `mode: "direct"`)

**Interfaces:**
- Readiness: the step captures `_codex_lane_mode <id>` first (rc 1 ends the step with no event, as an unmeasured
  tier does today) and walks `_codex_mode_tiers "$mode"`. On a `litellm` lane it is today's question exactly, the
  check-only comparison included. On a `direct` lane it is the bridge tier alone, answering as itself (its
  `direct`-shaped `/ccgpt/lane` and the pid its argv proves, Task 11), through the same bounded read, and no LiteLLM
  check runs.
- The canary: `laneInputs` also reads `lane.json`'s `bridge`, and `laneOf` accepts the `shim` shape on a `litellm`
  lane and the `direct` shape (`{"lane": <string>, "mode": "direct"}`, exactly) on a `direct` lane, each only when it
  names this lane; an absent or unknown `bridge` is `unavailable` with no keyed request, as `_codex_lane_mode`
  refuses one. The request and the reader are unchanged.

### Task 12: `ccrc codex switch <id> litellm|direct`

**Files:**
- Modify: `ccd/ccrc` (`cmd_codex` `:13493-13527`, a new `_codex_cmd_switch`, the usage text); `deploy/account-op.mjs`
  (an `OPS` row `codex-mode` and its op)
- Test: `server/test/ccrc-codex.test.ts`, `server/test/ccrc-cli.test.ts` (`:211`), `server/test/account-ops.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `account-op codex-mode --file <roster> --id <id> --bridge litellm|direct` — read-modify-write through
  `writeRoster`, refusing a non-codex row, an unknown mode and an unreadable roster; it writes `exec.bridge` only
  when the mode is `direct` and DELETES the key for `litellm`, so a lane switched back reads exactly as it did
  before this plan. `ccrc codex switch <id> litellm|direct`, in this order, under `_codex_lock` from start to end:
  1. `_codex_row`; refuse `upstream-unsupported` for `siwc`; `already` (rc 0, nothing touched) when the lane is
     already in that mode and its tiers are up.
  2. Stop the outgoing set through `_codex_stop_tier` (going to `direct`: `shim` then `litellm`; going to `litellm`:
     `bridge`), capturing each word; `foreign` stops the switch with the lane named down and nothing written.
  3. Re-measure every tier as not running (rc 1 of `_codex_tier_ours`); a running one stops the switch.
  4. Record the mode (`codex-mode`), then re-render `lane.json` UNCONDITIONALLY (`_models_node_locked <id>
     materialise`, not `_codex_lane_json_ensure`, whose presence check reads a re-moded file as current), then
     re-measure: `_codex_lane_mode <id>` must print the new mode.
  5. Start the incoming set by running the start verb in a subshell, `( _codex_cmd_start "$id" ); rc=$?`, because
     `_codex_cmd_start` exits (`ccd/ccrc:13175` and on), and read its rc; its own `_codex_lock` call is a no-op under
     the lock this shell holds (`_codex_lock`'s ONE LEVEL rule, `:12796-12802`), and the subshell's exit closes only
     its copy of the fd, which is not a release.
  6. On a failure at 4 or 5, the lane is left down and named, with the remedy `ccrc codex switch <id> <the old
     mode>`; nothing is restarted behind the operator's back.
  Between steps 3 and 5 no refresher runs, so the credential changes hands without a race (spec §2.1).
- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `'switch to direct stops both tiers, records the mode and starts the
  bridge'`; `'switch back deletes exec.bridge and starts both tiers'` (the roster bytes equal the pre-switch bytes);
  `'a foreign listener stops the switch before anything is written'`; `'a tier that will not stop leaves the lane
  down and named'`; `'switch to the mode it is in is already'`; `'switch takes the lane lock'`; `'switch refuses
  siwc'`; `'a failed start leaves the lane down and names the way back'` (the incoming tier refuses: the switch
  prints its own step-6 line with `ccrc codex switch <id> <old mode>` and exits non-zero); `'the switch re-renders
  lane.json and reads the new mode back'`; the usage list in `ccrc-cli.test.ts` names `switch`.
- [ ] **Steps 2-4.** - [ ] **Step 5: Mutation rows** — W1 (start before the stop) → 2; W2 (no re-measure) → 1; W3

### Task 15: D25 — a refusal lowers the window through the hourly refresh

**Files:**
- Modify: `deploy/models-op.mjs` (a new op `lower-windows`, its `OPS` row with `writes: true`; reuse
  `readWindowsFile`, `writeWindows`, `renderRefusal`, `materialise`); `ccd/ccrc` (`MODELS_WRITE_OPS` `:2355` gains
  `lower-windows`; the refresh loop gains a lowering pass of its own BEFORE `_models_refresh_one` (`:11767`): the
  materialise at `:11784` sits inside the probe-success branch, whose `else` is the failed probe at `:11830`)
- Test: `server/test/models-op.test.ts`, `server/test/ccrc-models.test.ts`, `server/test/ccrc-models-lock.test.ts`
  (`:664`, a bare call of a write op is refused), `server/test/single-definition.test.ts` (`:7639`, `MODELS_WRITE_OPS`
  against the `writes: true` rows), `server/test/modelenv.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `models-op lower-windows --file <registry> --id <id> --stats <abs path>` → `{ok: true, lowered:
  [{model, from, to, refused}], skipped: [{model, why}]}`. It reads the lane's upstream from the parsed roster
  (Task 10) and takes only `refusals["<upstream>/<model>"]` and `routes["<upstream>/<model>"]`, so a refusal on one
  route never lowers another. For each such refusal:
  0. **A refusal is applied once.** When its `at` is not later than the windows file's top-level
     `refusalsApplied["<upstream>/<model>"]` → skipped `already-applied`. So after `clear-window` undoes a lowering,
     the next refresh does not lower again from the same refusal, which the stats file keeps for good (Task 5).
  1. `to = floor(tokens × 0.9 / 1000) × 1000`; below `WINDOW_MIN` → skipped `below-minimum`.
  2. **The floor of real usage:** `to` is raised to `ceil(promptTokensMax / 1000) × 1000` of that route and model,
     the largest prompt the model accepted in real usage (Task 5), because `tokens` is an estimate (D24) and an
     under-estimate would otherwise lower the lane below what it has served
     (D-6428).
  3. A model no class names and no catalogue entry lists → skipped `not-a-lane-model` (never a failure).
  4. With an entry: only when the refusal's `at` is later than the entry's `setAt`, and `to` is smaller; otherwise
     skipped `not-lower` or `older-than-entry`, so a deliberate `set-window` after a refusal is never undone.
  5. With no entry: `to` is capped at the lane's current `contextWindowFor(...).tokens`, so the lane's window never
     rises (D-6421).
  6. Writes `{tokens: to, setAt: now, source: "refusal", refusedTokensEstimate: tokens}` (the name says the refused
     size is the bridge's estimate; unknown keys are tolerated by `parseWindows`) and records the refusal it applied,
     `refusalsApplied["<upstream>/<model>"] = at`, in the same write, then re-materialises, once, after all models.
     Measured, nothing else changes for the record: `parseWindows` tolerates an unknown top-level key
     (`shared/models.mjs:497-500`, "this file TOLERATES them, at the top level"), and `clear-window` keeps one: it
     edits a clone of the raw file (`deploy/models-op.mjs:1359`), deletes only the model's entry (`:1370`), and
     removes the file only when `windows` is empty and no other top-level key exists (`:1376`), else writes it back
     (D-5161). `set-window` writes the same clone, less the refusal's `source` and `refusedTokensEstimate` (D-6905),
     and `lower-windows` writes through it too (`writeWindows`).
  Every refusal the pass decides at steps 1-6 is recorded the same way, `refusalsApplied["<upstream>/<model>"] = at`,
  whether it lowered the window or was skipped (`below-minimum`, `not-a-lane-model`, `not-lower`, `older-than-entry`),
  in the pass's one write, so each refusal is read once: a refusal skipped `older-than-entry` or `not-lower` is not
  applied by a later refresh after `clear-window` removes the entry that skipped it.
  The refresh runs it for each `direct` lane whose `lane.json` names a stats file, under `_models_node_locked`, in
  its own pass before the probe, so a failed probe never skips a lowering.
- The compaction chain's two env facts, pinned in `modelenv.test.ts`: `modelEnvBlock` never emits
  `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_UNKNOWN_MODEL_WINDOW_ENFORCEMENT` (with it set, Claude Code compacts proactively at no size,
  `window/compaction.md` "In short" 3) nor `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` (it lowers the threshold below window −
  33k, "In short" 4; a value above the window is clamped). Today the first rule is a comment only
  (`shared/modelenv.mjs:30-33`).
- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `'a refusal at 380000 sets the window to 342000'`; `'a refusal never
  raises a window'`; `'a set-window after the refusal is not undone'`; `'a first entry is capped at the lane's
  window'`; `'a refusal of 1100 tokens is skipped below-minimum'`; `'a model no class names is skipped'`; `'a
  refusal estimated low never lowers below the largest accepted prompt'` (refusal 120000, `promptTokensMax` 281500:
  `to` is 282000); `'a refusal on another route is not read'` (`refusals["siwc/<m>"]` on a backend lane: nothing
  lowered); `'the entry records refusedTokensEstimate'`; `'a refusal lowers, clear-window removes it, and the next
  refresh does not lower again'` (`refusalsApplied` survives the clear, and the second pass answers
  `already-applied`); `'a refusal skipped older-than-entry, then set-window and clear-window, is not applied on the
  next refresh'` (the refusal is recorded at its skip, and the pass after the clear answers `already-applied`); `'the
  hourly refresh lowers before the probe, and a failed
  probe does not stop it'`; `'the new window reaches settings.json in the same run'`; `'lower-windows is a write op
  and refused outside the lane-files lock'` (`ccrc-models-lock.test.ts`); `'no lane env block names the
  enforcement switch or the auto-compact window'` (`modelenv.test.ts`, over every class layout the suite builds).
- [ ] **Steps 2-4.** - [ ] **Step 5: Mutation rows** — Z1 (× 1.0) → 1; Z2 (raise when larger) → 1; Z3 (ignore
  `setAt`) → 1; Z4 (no first-entry cap) → 1; Z5 (the pass inside the probe-success branch) → 1 (… a failed probe
  does not stop it); Z6 (no usage floor) → 1; Z7 (read refusals by model only) → 1; Z8 (`writes: false`) → 1
  (single-definition); Z9 (`modelEnvBlock` emits the enforcement switch) → 1; Z10 (drop the applied record: no

# Wave 3 — the drift watch, the replay, and the live-step tools (one PR)

**What it delivers:** D3's doctor warning, D4's replay guarantee, D1's Codex-source watch, and the two tools the
first live step uses: recordings with a scrubber, and a bounded live probe.
**Effect on a live box:** a WARN line when a `direct` lane met an unexplained name; nothing else runs unasked.

### Task 17: D3 — the doctor warns on an unexplained name

**Files:**
- Modify: `ccd/ccrc-doctor-checks` (inside `_check_codex`'s per-lane loop)
- Test: `server/test/ccrc-doctor.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the stats file's `unexplained` (Task 5).
- Produces: one WARN per `direct` lane, `<id>: the bridge met <n> name(s) it does not know: <family>:<name>, …
  (names only, never content). Each is a protocol change to read; it is explained by adding it to
  ccd/codexbridge/known.py in a reviewed change.` A name is explained only in code, so the 7-day watch's "no
  unexplained new name" is a doctor line, not a judgement. The rollout of that change restarts the bridge, whose
  load drops the name from the carried list (Task 5), so the WARN clears.
- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `'a direct lane with an unexplained event type warns, naming it'`;
  `'a known name never warns'`; `'a litellm lane has no drift row'`; `'the WARN line holds names only'` (a stats file
  whose `unexplained` holds a sentinel outside `NAME_RE` prints `unrecognised`).
- [ ] Steps 2-6 as above. Mutation rows K1 (no WARN) → 1; K2 (WARN on a known name) → 1.

### Task 18: recordings, the scrubber and the live probe

**Files:**
- Create: `ccd/codexbridge/record.py`, `ccd/codexbridge/liveprobe.py`
- Modify: `ccd/codexbridge/server.py` (the recording hook)
- Test: `server/test/codex-bridge-live-tools.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  - While `record-streams` exists in the lane directory, the bridge writes each upstream response's raw bytes to
    `recordings/<utc>-<n>.sse` (0600, directory 0700), at most `RECORD_MAX = 20` files, then stops recording.
  - Both CLIs run as files under `-I` (which keeps the current directory off `sys.path`, so `-m` cannot find the
    package): each sets `sys.dont_write_bytecode = True`, then puts its own package's parent directory at
    `sys.path[0]` and nothing else.
  - `python3 -I <tree>/ccd/codexbridge/record.py scrub <in.sse> <out.sse>` — replaces every id with `<prefix>_fx<n>`,
    every `encrypted_content` with `ENC-<n>`, every text with a fixed synthetic string of the same structure, and
    drops every header line; refuses (rc 1) when the output still matches any of the scrub patterns. Its output is a
    corpus stream (Task 3's format), with its expectation written by `make_streams.py`'s `--from-recording`.
  - `python3 -I <tree>/ccd/codexbridge/liveprobe.py --lane <id> --step <1-6> [--dry-run]` — the first live step's six
    requests (the runbook's list), each tiny, `originator: ccrc`, no `instructions`; reads the credential with Stage
    1's `read` only (an expired token refuses `token-expired`, it never refreshes in `litellm` mode); refuses unless
    `--lane`'s lane directory holds the operator's `liveprobe-allowed` marker; counts its requests in
    `liveprobe.count` and refuses past 6; records the raw stream; prints only status, request id, event and item type
    names, `end_turn`, the usage fields and the response header names. `--dry-run` prints the body it would send.
- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** (against `fakeResponses` only) — `'recording stops after RECORD_MAX'`;
  `'the scrubber leaves no id, encrypted content or text of the original'`; `'the scrubber refuses an output that
  still matches'`; `'the live probe refuses without the marker, past six requests, and on an expired token'`; `'the
  live probe never sends instructions and sends originator ccrc'`; `'the live probe prints no body text'`.
- [ ] Steps 2-6. Mutation rows Q1 (no request cap) → 1; Q2 (scrub keeps `encrypted_content`) → 1; Q3 (refresh in the
  probe) → 1.

### Task 19: D1 — the Codex source watch, and D4's replay guarantee

**Files:**
- Create: `.github/workflows/codex-wire-watch.yml`, `.github/ci/codex-wire-watch.mjs`,
  `server/test/codex-wire-watch.test.ts`, `server/test/fixtures/codex-wire/` (synthetic excerpts)
- Test: `server/test/codex-wire-watch.test.ts`, `server/test/ci-select-tests.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `wireReport(before: Record<string, string>, after: Record<string, string>): {changed: string[],
  findings: string[]}` over the nine contract files (`codex-api/src/common.rs`, `codex-api/src/sse/responses.rs`,
  `codex-api/src/sse/responses_error.rs`, `codex-api/src/api_bridge.rs`, `core/src/client.rs`,
  `core/src/context/base_instructions.rs`, `protocol/src/models.rs`, `protocol/src/openai_models.rs`,
  `core/src/responses_retry.rs`); findings name a removed or added request-struct field, event literal, error code
  literal or header literal. The workflow runs daily (spec D1: "on each Codex stable release"): it lists the stable
  `rust-v*` releases of `openai/codex`, reads the last WALKED tag from one state issue, `codex wire: state`, whose
  body is that tag alone, kept apart from the finding issues; and for every CONSECUTIVE pair of stable tags from that
  tag on, oldest first, fetches the nine files at each and opens one issue per pair with findings (titled `codex
  wire: <old>..<new>`, skipped when that title exists) listing them and the upstream compare link. A pair with no
  finding opens nothing. A pair counts as walked only when all nine files were fetched at both of its tags; the first
  pair that could not be compared (a fetch error, a rate limit, a contract file missing at one tag) ends the walk,
  the state is left at the last pair compared, never past the one that failed, and the job exits non-zero. After the
  walk, the state issue's body becomes the newest tag walked (the issue is created on the first run), so a
  finding-free pair is never walked twice. With no state issue yet, the walk starts at the
  second-newest stable tag (the newest pair). So every release between two runs is compared, never skipped, whether
  or not any pair has had a finding. The issue text holds nothing but upstream names. `nextPairs(stableTags:
  string[], lastWalked: string | null): Array<[string, string]>` is exported for the test.
- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** — `'it reports the removal of the instructions field'` and `'it reports the
  removal of the compaction endpoint constant'` (synthetic excerpts shaped like the two upstream changes, 0.162.0
  and 0.155.0, D-6424); `'an unchanged pair reports nothing'`; `'three
  releases since the last walk give three consecutive pairs, oldest first'`, `'no report yet, two releases since the
  last walk give both pairs'` and `'with no state yet, the newest pair'` (`nextPairs`); `'a fetch failure on the
  second of three pairs leaves the state at the first pair's newer tag'` (and the job exits non-zero); and in
  `ci-select-tests.test.ts`, `'a change under ccd/codexbridge selects the bridge suites'` (a map built from a trace
  record that lists `ccd/codexbridge/reducer.py` as read selects `codex-bridge-replay.test.ts`), which is D4: every
  bridge test reads the whole package through `load_all()`, so the traced selection runs the replay corpus on every
  bridge change, and the daily full run backs it.
- [ ] Steps 2-6. Mutation rows G1 (ignore removed fields) → 1; G2 (open an issue on an unchanged pair) → 1; G3
  (`nextPairs`: the newest pair only) → 2 (three releases since the last walk …; no report yet, two releases …); G4
  (advance the state past a pair that could not be compared) → 1 (a fetch failure on the second of three pairs …).

## Operator runbook: the first live step, the flip, the watch (not tasks)

Nothing here is run by an agent unless the operator says so for that step. Wave 2 changes nothing until step 4.

**0. Offline rehearsal (an agent may run it; zero network).** In a fixture HOME, Claude Code's versioned binary is run
directly (never the `~/.local/bin` launcher, which sources a token file from `$HOME`), under `env -i` with a dummy
key, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1`, the lane's settings env block and `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES=2`,
against a fixture lane's bridge whose upstream is a fake Responses backend on loopback, with the fake also set as
`HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` (it must record zero CONNECT requests). Expected: a tool round trip; proactive compaction
at window − 33k with the fake's usage in `message_delta`; the prompt-too-long 400 after one exchange starting a
compaction; a 429 `billing_error` with `x-should-retry: false` ending the turn with one request; a `policy_blocked`
400 with one request; a mid-stream `error` followed by a served `stream:false` re-send; pings through a 60 s silence;
and five reactions nothing has measured yet:
- an overflow `response.failed` delivered AFTER `HOLD_S` committed the head (an `error` event carrying the
  prompt-too-long text): expected, the `stream:false` re-send, a real 400 prompt-too-long, then a compaction;
- a 310 s upstream silence with the bridge's pings: Claude Code survives it (its stream watchdog floors at 300 s,
  `reply.md` R18; whether a ping resets it is the question), and the turn then ends on the bridge's own idle error;
- a 15 s held head with no bytes (`HOLD_S`): Claude Code waits for it (its first-byte timeout default was not read,
  Open question 3);
- a lane-shape refusal: `stop_reason: refusal` twice, so Claude Code's fallback path runs (`stub.md` runs 02/03 were
  the Opus shape); expected, any non-catalogue model it sends is answered 404 locally;
- an assistant text with trailing whitespace and non-ASCII characters is sent back byte for byte in the next
  request (text-only reasoning replay keys on it, Task 3's R14).
Any difference is reported before step 1.

**1. Choose the moment.** lane-b, the lane with more quota headroom (spec §2.8), at a quiet time; its build carries
waves 1 and 2; `ccrc doctor` is clean for it; its access token is fresh (`ccrc codex status lane-b --json | jq
.auth`), because the live probe never refreshes in `litellm` mode.

**2. The first live step: at most six tiny requests** (D27; spec §5). `touch ~/.ccrc/codex/lane-b/liveprobe-allowed`,
then `CCGPT_CCRC_VERSION="$(ccrc version | sed -n 's/^version //p')" python3 -I ~/ccrc/ccd/codexbridge/liveprobe.py --lane lane-b
--step <n>`, one at a time. The prefix gives the probe the version the bridge's start sends, so step 1 measures the
flip's User-Agent: `ccrc version` prints a `version` line only when the box's stamp names one, and without it the probe
sends `dev`, as the bridge does:
1. a one-word reply: `originator: ccrc` accepted, developer-first with no `instructions`, the usage fields, the
   response header names (`x-codex-*`, `openai-model`, `x-request-id`);
2. two parallel tool calls with `parallel_tool_calls: true` and top-level tools;
3. the round trip of step 2 with a `toolu_`-shaped id and the reasoning item replayed (with and without its `rs_`
   id, if step 3 is refused with it);
4. a request whose last item is a developer message after the user message (a turn's first request);
5. step 1 repeated with the same `session-id` and `prompt_cache_key`: `cached_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens`;
6. spare, for re-running one that failed for a reason not about the request.
Then `rm` the marker. If request 1 is refused for its originator or user agent, STOP: the lane stays on `litellm`
until the operator rules (D27). Scrub the recordings into the corpus (`record.py scrub`), in a reviewed
change.

**3. Read the answers against the plan's open questions** (tools at top level, the trailing developer item, the
reasoning id, the cache headers, and the order of a mixed turn: step 3's replay is reasoning, message, reasoning,
call when step 2's reply carried text before its calls) and record them in this plan's Deviations or the Stage 3
plan.

**4. The flip.** `ccrc codex switch lane-b direct`. Verify: `ccrc codex status lane-b --json` reads `mode: direct`,
`tiers.bridge` running, `tiers.litellm` and `tiers.shim` not running; `ccrc doctor` keeps the `codex` check green;
a session's next turn answers; `bridge.stats.json` moves.

**5. The 7-day watch** (spec §2.8), with lane-b's own 7 days on `litellm` before the flip as the baseline:
- zero empty tool calls: `outcomes.emptyArguments` stays 0 (the reducer counts a released `tool_use` whose input is
  `{}` for a tool whose schema has required properties, Task 3's R8), and no session reports an empty `tool_use`
  input;
- a 5xx rate (`outcomes.5xx` / requests) no worse than the baseline's (from `litellm.log`, counts only); a stream whose client
  went away (`outcomes.client-gone`) and one a restart's drain cut (`outcomes.drain-cut`) are not upstream failures and are
  not counted `5xx` (D-6835; only the handler's unexpected-exception arm still counts one), so they stay out of this rate;
- a compaction rate no worse than the baseline's, measured the SAME way for both weeks from lane-b's transcripts, as
  the spec measured its 2.35% (§1.1): automatic `compact_boundary` records over assistant calls, deduplicated by
  record id and attributed by the model that answered (`outcomes.compactionRequests` is a cross-check, not the
  measure: a `litellm` lane has no such counter);
- no credential incident: the status never reads `needs-signin` or `config-error` without a cause the operator
  knows, no `refresh_token_reused`, `lastRefreshAt` moving about every half token lifetime;
- no unexplained name in the doctor's WARN (Task 17) at the end of the week.
D25 may lower a model's window during the week; each lowering is in `ccrc models lane-b show --json`'s windows with
`source: "refusal"` and its `refusedTokensEstimate`, and the operator decides whether to keep it. A lowering applies
to sessions started after it; whether a running session re-reads `settings.json` is unmeasured.

**6. Flip back** at any failed criterion, or at once on a credential incident: `ccrc codex switch lane-b litellm`.
The roster then reads as it did before the flip. **Before any rollback** to a build without the bridge, switch every
`direct` lane back first. What happens otherwise, measured from the code: the older build reads the bridge's lane
answer as `listener-unidentified` (foreign, Task 11), so its `ccrc codex start`, which every session launch on the
lane runs, refuses "ccrc never adopts or stops what it cannot identify" and starts nothing (`ccd/ccrc:13218-13225`),
and its `stop` leaves the bridge running. The running bridge keeps serving the sessions already open (it imported
its package at start), but no new session on that lane starts. An unattended rollback (the update watchdog's)
lands here with nobody to switch first; the way out is `ccrc update --to <a build with the bridge>`, then `ccrc codex
switch lane-b litellm`. LiteLLM is never started beside the bridge, so the credential still has one refresher.

**7. After the week**: the result, the counters and the decision go in this plan's Deviations (or a ledger PR); lane-a
follows only by the operator's ruling, and LiteLLM's removal is Stage 4.

## Known limits and Later

- `clear_tool_uses_20250919` is counted, not applied: Claude Code 2.1.308 sends it only when a flag-gated idle
  feature is on (D-6417); the doctor's WARN names it the first time.
- The canary (D5, D26) needs consent (D22), so it is Stage 3's (D-6423).
- A Claude Code drift watch (re-running the offline stub on each Claude Code release) is Later: no CI job can run
  the binary.
- The trimming of what Claude Code re-attaches after a compaction stays the spec's Later.
- Tools travel at top level, as today; the Codex CLI's Responses Lite form for these model ids (`additional_tools`
  with a `functions` namespace, `core/src/client.rs:915-927`) is an A/B for after the watch.
- The WebSocket transport, `text.verbosity`, `reasoning.summary` and zstd bodies are not used.
- Spec §5's `siwc` items (namespace replay, every SIWC error code before and during the stream) are Stage 3's, with
  the `siwc` adapter.
- **Holding the port across a restart** (systemd socket activation, or passing the listener to a successor) is out
  of scope: a restart refuses connections between the old listener's close and the new bind (Review Focus 3).
- **No suite runs on a Python 3.9 interpreter.** The floor is held by the census (grammar at 3.9, the PEP 604 walk,
  the stdlib allowlist, no bare `except TimeoutError`), as Stage 1's is; a 3.9-only runtime difference outside those
  is caught on the first macOS box that runs it.
- **The systemd stop's bound is the manager's default.** A box whose user manager sets `DefaultTimeoutStopSec`
  below `DRAIN_S + STOP_MARGIN_S` (10 s) cuts the drain early; nothing measures it.
- **An idle lane's stats file can lag its counts (D-6998).** The bridge has no trailing flush: counts made while a
  write was in flight, or within `FLUSH_S` of the last write, stay in memory until the next request or a graceful
  stop's drain, and a SIGKILL or an OOM kill drops them. The 7-day watch reads its counters after a request, or after
  a restart's drain, never from an idle lane's file alone.

## Open questions

1. **N and M in the overflow text.** The backend's overflow message carries no counts, so N is the bridge's
   estimate and M the lower of the lane's window and 0.9 N. Claude Code trims by them; one real overflow recording
   will show whether the trim is sensible (D-6415).
2. **The window the backend enforces** moves over time. It was measured once: on 2026-10-15 every class model
   accepted about 511k through one lane on the backend route, with no refusal (spec §1.2, D7); the lanes are told
   400k. D25 is the standing learner from here, so the first live step stays at six tiny requests and probes no
   window.
3. **The 15 s held head** against Claude Code's first-byte timeout, whose default was not read; runbook step 0 runs
   it once offline.
4. **Effort.** Claude Code sends an explicit effort on every request, so `ccrc models set-effort`'s lane default never
   applies on this traffic (today too). Keep Claude Code's, or let the lane default win?
5. **The continuation cap** of 3 for `end_turn:false`: enough, or should the bridge stop at 1 until the watch shows
   how often it happens?
6. **Stage 1's contract.** This plan adds `refresh_rejected`, `record_upstream` and `half_life_s` to the library
   (Task 6) and a third status writer; should Stage 1's plan name them before its wave 1 lands instead?

## Deviations found

Every entry carries the number the controller issued (D-6410 to D-6431 at plan time, D-6804 to D-6839 for wave 1,
D-6862 to D-6907 and D-6921 for wave 2, D-6944 to D-6963 for wave 3, D-6994 to D-6998 for the follow-up to the
suite's macOS failures, D-7083 to D-7089, D-7093, D-7094, D-7119 and D-7120 for what the first live step, the flip and the rollout measured). A further
departure found while executing is written `D-TBD-<slug>` and named in the task's report.

### D-6410 — `end_turn:false` is continued upstream, never `pause_turn`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "A reply the model marks as not finished (`end_turn:false`) becomes `pause_turn`", and
  "an interruption a normal stop".
- **What changed:** a reply ending `end_turn:false`, or `response.incomplete` `interrupted`, with no `tool_use`, is
  continued upstream inside the same Anthropic message, at most 3 times (Task 8); `pause_turn` is never emitted.
- **Why:** measured: Claude Code 2.1.308 does not continue a `pause_turn` (one request, the turn ends, the partial text
  is the result). The Codex CLI continues itself (`core/src/session/turn.rs:2988-2990` @ `092d3ac`) and maps
  `interrupted` to `end_turn:false` (`codex-api/src/sse/responses.rs:418-455`), so the spec's two sentences collide.
- **Cost if wrong:** up to three more upstream requests for one reply, counted; an unbounded need ends `end_turn`.

### D-6411 — 400 `policy_blocked` with `x-should-retry: false`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "Policy refusal | 400, not retried", which names no error type or header.
- **What changed:** `error.type` `policy_blocked`, header `x-should-retry: false`, before and after commit.
- **Why:** measured: a plain 400 makes Claude Code retry three times with features turned off; its embedded gateway
  contract names this shape.
- **Cost if wrong:** a client older than the contract may read `policy_blocked` as an unknown type; it still sees 400.

### D-6412 — 429 `billing_error`, `retry-after`, `x-should-retry: false`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "Usage limit (`…_usage_limit_exceeded`) | 429 `rate_limit_error`".
- **What changed:** the backend's code is `usage_limit_reached` (HTTP only) plus the quota codes; the answer is 429
  `billing_error`, `retry-after` from `resets_at`, `x-should-retry: false`; the status reads `limit-paused`.
- **Why:** measured: without the header Claude Code retries (2 on a lane, 10 by default, about 3 minutes); the
  embedded contract names `billing_error` for a plan limit and keeps `rate_limit_error` for throttling.
- **Cost if wrong:** a session fails its turn at once on a limit instead of after the retries; that is the intent.

### D-6413 — throttling is a row of its own

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4's table, which has no row for `rate_limit_exceeded`, `slow_down` or another 429.
- **What changed:** 429 `rate_limit_error`, the upstream `retry-after` passed through, no `x-should-retry`, so Claude
  Code retries as it does for an Anthropic throttle. `slow_down` arrives as an HTTP 503 and is read by its code.
- **Why:** Claude Code 2.1.308's embedded gateway contract keeps `rate_limit_error` for throttling and gives
  `billing_error` to a plan limit; a throttle is worth a retry, a plan limit is not.
- **Cost if wrong:** two retries a turn on a lane, as today.

### D-6414 — an unmapped feature is `capability_rejected: <class>`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "Unsupported capability | 400 naming the field".
- **What changed:** the message is `capability_rejected: <class>` from Claude Code's class table (`document_block`,
  `image_block`, `structured_outputs_unsupported`, …), with the field name after it; a server tool, which has no class,
  is a plain 400 naming its type with `x-should-retry: false`.
- **Why:** Claude Code self-heals only on those tokens; a field name alone gets the generic degrade retries.
- **Cost if wrong:** none on the backend route today: no such request was seen.

### D-6415 — where the prompt-too-long numbers come from

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, which gives the text and not its numbers, and its "Claude Code 2.1.300 parses that
  text" (2.1.308's match is a loose substring, measured).
- **What changed:** `N` is the D24 estimate of the refused request, `M` the lower of the lane's window (its
  `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS`) and `floor(0.9 N / 1000) × 1000`, so `N > M` always; the refusal is recorded at `N`
  for D25.
- **Why:** the backend's overflow message has no counts (`codex-api/src/sse/responses.rs:1137` @ `092d3ac`).
- **Cost if wrong:** Claude Code trims by the pair; an over-estimate drops more history before its summary.

### D-6416 — `cache_write_tokens` is reported as cache creation

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "Input is reported net of cached tokens, and cached tokens as cache reads", which says
  nothing of cache writes.
- **What changed:** `input_tokens = input − cached − cache_write`, `cache_creation_input_tokens = cache_write`, so the
  three fields Claude Code sums equal the upstream `input_tokens`, and proactive compaction fires at window − 33k
  (measured threshold rule).
- **Why:** measured offline: Claude Code's count includes `cache_creation_input_tokens` (`window/compaction.md`, "In
  short" 5), so reporting the cache write there keeps the sum equal to what the upstream counted.
- **Cost if wrong:** if `cache_write_tokens` is not inside `input_tokens`, the count is low by it; the first live step
  reads a non-zero one (step 2.5).

### D-6417 — no `clear_tool_uses` in the bridge

- **Departs from:** spec §2.3, "`context_management` | applied by the bridge itself (`clear_tool_uses`)".
- **What changed:** every edit type is counted; none is applied.
- **Why:** measured: 74 of 74 requests carried only `clear_thinking_20251015` with `keep:"all"`, which removes
  nothing; `clear_tool_uses_20250919` is a flag-gated idle feature in 2.1.308.
- **Cost if wrong:** if that flag turns on, tool results are not cleared on a `direct` lane (LiteLLM polyfilled the
  edit); the doctor names the edit the first day it arrives.

### D-6418 — D3's names go to the stats file

- **Departs from:** spec §2.9 D3, "They go to the status file".
- **What changed:** names, counts and the unexplained list live in `bridge.stats.json`; the status file keeps the
  credential's state, the code and the request id.
- **Why:** Stage 1's status file is written only under the credential's lock; counters updated on every request would
  hold that lock in the request path.
- **Cost if wrong:** one more non-secret file per lane, reaped with the lane.

### D-6419 — the package is imported from the installed tree

- **Departs from:** D18, "a stdlib package beside" the shim.
- **What changed:** the package stays at `<tree>/ccd/codexbridge/`; the shim, placed in `~/.local/bin`, imports it from
  there in `direct` mode, and the bridge's started record hashes it, so an update restarts a running bridge.
- **Why:** every placement path places single files (Stage 1's D-6376); the tree is already the
  box's code (`ccrc` runs `deploy/models-op.mjs` from it, `ccd/ccrc:1326`), and on the release lane it is immutable.
- **Cost if wrong:** a `deploy.sh` push rewrites the tree in place; a running bridge imported everything at start.

### D-6420 — D11's per-lane switch

- **Departs from:** D11, which names a per-lane switch and no mechanism.
- **What changed:** a marker file, `bridge-instructions`, in the lane directory, with no writer in the tree; with it,
  `system` travels as `instructions` (Claude Code's text alone, never LiteLLM's prompt).
- **Why:** no roster field until the developer-first shape and its switch are proven on a live lane; a field is an
  additive change to two validators, the mirror and the README, made once it is known to be needed.
- **Cost if wrong:** the operator touches a file instead of a roster field; a later stage may promote it.

### D-6421 — D25 on a model with no entry

- **Departs from:** D25, "sets that model's window to R × 0.9 … never raises", read per entry.
- **What changed:** a first automatic entry is capped at the lane's current window, and a refusal older than an
  entry's `setAt` is ignored (Task 15).
- **Why:** with every other class model measured, an uncapped first entry would raise the lane from the 200k default;
  and an old refusal would undo a deliberate `set-window`.
- **Cost if wrong:** a window learned from a refusal can end below what the backend accepts; raising is `set-window`.

### D-6422 — the bridge writes upstream states into the status file

- **Departs from:** Stage 1's contract: the status file "is written only under the lock, by `ensure_fresh` and
  `sign_in_device`".
- **What changed:** `record_upstream` (Task 6) is a third writer, under the same lock, merging only `state`
  (`limit-paused`, `ineligible`, and `ok` over them), `lastErrorCode` and `lastRequestId`.
- **Why:** spec §2.4 puts `limit-paused`, `ineligible` and the upstream request id in the lane's status.
- **Cost if wrong:** a two-second lock wait at most, best effort, after a reply is sent.

### D-6423 — no canary in Stage 2

- **Departs from:** D26 ("during the soak … the canary runs `ccrc codex switch <id> litellm`").
- **What changed:** no canary runs in Stage 2, so the soak has no automatic flip back.
- **Why:** D5 runs the canary "under the lane's consent", which is D22, Stage 3; the 7-day watch's flip back is the
  operator's (runbook step 6).
- **Cost if wrong:** a bad day on lane-b is caught by the doctor and the operator, not by a canary.

### D-6424 — D1's own test

- **Departs from:** D1, "Its own test checks that it reports the 0.162.0 move of `instructions` and the 0.155.0
  removal of the compaction endpoint", read as the real files.
- **What changed:** the test runs on short synthetic excerpts shaped like those two changes; the workflow reads the
  real files at run time.
- **Why:** tests run offline, and no upstream source is copied into this repository.
- **Cost if wrong:** a real-file shape the excerpts miss is found by the first real run, which opens an issue.

### D-6425 — `output_config.format` becomes the Responses `text.format`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.3, which has no structured-output row ("Anything unknown | passed or dropped by rule").
- **What changed:** the shim's effort hook pops `output_config` before inbound sees the body
  (`ccd/ccgpt-proxy.py:575-577`), so the direct entry hands the popped value to inbound. A `format` of type
  `json_schema` is sent as `text: {"format": {"type": "json_schema", "strict": true, "schema": …, "name":
  "ccrc_output_schema"}}`, after `prompt_cache_key`; any other format is `capability_rejected:
  structured_outputs_unsupported`; every other key but `effort` is counted `requestField:output_config.<key>`.
- **Why:** it is the shape the Codex CLI sends for an output schema (`codex-api/src/common.rs:196-223, 392-410`,
  `core/src/client.rs:973-977` @ `092d3ac`; `strict` true is the `Prompt` default, `core/src/client_common.rs:52`),
  with the bridge's own name, as D27 names the bridge and never Codex. Claude Code 2.1.308 sent no `format` in the
  offline runs (`--json-schema` uses a `StructuredOutput` tool, `stub.md` run 25), so this row is for a feature that
  may send one.
- **Cost if wrong:** a schema the backend's strict mode rejects comes back as a 400, which reaches Claude Code as the
  generic 4xx row; the first one names the code in the stats file.

### D-6426 — the sampling fields are dropped on the backend route too

- **Departs from:** spec §2.3, "`max_tokens`, `temperature`, `top_p`, `top_k`, `metadata`, `stop_sequences` | dropped
  on `siwc`; counted".
- **What changed:** they are dropped and counted on the backend route as well.
- **Why:** the Codex CLI never sends `max_output_tokens` or a sampling field on this route
  (`core/src/client.rs:891-1014` @ `092d3ac`), and the first live step sends none, so nothing vouches for the backend
  accepting them.
- **Cost if wrong:** no output cap and no sampling control on a `direct` lane; Claude Code sends no sampling field
  (`stub.md`, the body-keys row) and its `max_tokens` is 32000 for a GPT id.

### D-6427 — an image counts a fixed 1,600 tokens in D24

- **Departs from:** D24, "a running bytes-to-tokens ratio per route and model", which says nothing of images.
- **What changed:** `countable_bytes` is the bytes the bridge sends, image data excluded; each image adds
  `IMAGE_TOKENS = 1600` to the estimate; a request that carried an image never calibrates the ratio.
- **Why:** base64 image bytes are not tokens at 3.5 bytes each, and one screenshot in a session's history would drag
  the shared ratio toward its clamp, so later text-only estimates run low and D25 would learn a window from them.
- **Cost if wrong:** the allowance is unmeasured on this route; an image-heavy request is estimated off by the
  difference per image.

### D-6428 — D25 never lowers below a prompt the model accepted

- **Departs from:** D25, "A refusal at size R sets that model's window to R × 0.9, rounded down to 1k".
- **What changed:** R is the bridge's estimate, recorded as `refusedTokensEstimate`; the new window is at least the
  largest upstream `input_tokens` of a successful reply on that route and model (`promptTokensMax`), rounded up to
  1k.
- **Why:** the backend's overflow message carries no counts, so R is D24's estimate; an under-estimate would lower
  the whole lane far below what the model has served, and only a hand `set-window` would undo it.
- **Cost if wrong:** after a real shrink of the backend's limit below a size served earlier, the lane keeps a window
  above the new limit until the operator lowers it; each refusal still compacts through the prompt-too-long text.

### D-6429 — a restart cuts a stream longer than `DRAIN_S`

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4, "On restart the bridge stops taking requests and finishes the streams in flight".
- **What changed:** the drain lasts `DRAIN_S = 5.0` s; a stream still open then gets one `error` event `api_error`,
  which Claude Code answers with a `stream:false` re-send; every stop path waits at least 10 s before a KILL.
- **Why:** the bridge runs under the shim's unit, so nothing listens between the stop and the start, and Claude Code
  on a lane retries for about 1.7 s (`stub.md` run 24); a drain long enough to finish a GPT compaction (a median of
  239 to 271 s, spec §1.2) would refuse every new request for that long. A short drain costs at most a few seconds of
  refused connections per restart.
- **Cost if wrong:** a long stream in flight at a restart is re-sent whole (one more upstream turn), and a request
  landing in the gap can fail its turn; holding the port across a restart is a Known limit.

### D-6430 — the 24 synthetic streams stay until recordings replace them

- **Departs from:** spec §2.4 and §5, "the operator's trial recordings replace the other 24" (the reducer tests
  "re-based on the operator's trial recordings").
- **What changed:** the 24 are ported as synthetic, scrubbed streams (ruling 4); recordings from the first live step
  are scrubbed into the corpus later (runbook step 2, Task 18's scrubber).
- **Why:** the trial is Stage 3, and the live step's recordings arrive after wave 1 is merged.
- **Cost if wrong:** until then the corpus holds shapes no recording vouches for; a real shape they miss is found by
  the drift counters and the first recording.

### D-6431 — one `streamFalse` counter, not one per feature

- **Departs from:** D12, "with a counter per feature".
- **What changed:** `stream:false` requests are counted once, `outcomes.streamFalse`.
- **Why:** the offline runs found one sender only: the re-send after a mid-stream error (`stub.md` runs 06/07); there
  is no second feature to tell it from.
- **Cost if wrong:** a new `stream:false` sender is visible only as a rise in the one counter.

### D-6804 — the error table answers the overflow row without its numbers

- **Departs from:** The contract's error table (plan :314), whose answer before and after commit is exactly `prompt is
  too long: N tokens > M maximum`. Task 1's interface (plan :453) does not say who fills in N and M.
- **What changed:** `classify_http` and `classify_failed` answer the overflow row with kind `overflow`, code
  `context_length_exceeded`, 400 `invalid_request_error` and the bare text `prompt is too long`
  (`ccd/codexbridge/errors.py:199-202`, docstring :23-26). The server swaps in `overflow(N, M)` and keeps the upstream
  request id (`ccd/codexbridge/server.py:277-286`, called at :611-612).
- **Why:** The error table cannot know N (the D24 estimate) or M (the lane's window), and the backend's overflow message
  has no counts (D-6415). Only the server holds both numbers.
- **Cost if wrong:** A path that answers a classified overflow without going through `overflow_answer` sends the bare
  text. Claude Code's loose substring match still reads it as an overflow, but no refusal reaches the stats file for
  D25.

### D-6805 — `BridgeError` carries the row it came from

- **Departs from:** Task 1's interface (plan :448-449), which lists `BridgeError`'s fields as `status`, `etype`,
  `message`, `headers`, `state`, `code` and `request_id`.
- **What changed:** `BridgeError` gains `kind`: overflow, billing, rate, ineligible, policy, overloaded, unavailable,
  refused, auth or capability, with `refused` as the default (`ccd/codexbridge/errors.py:99-117`). The server uses it to
  tell the overflow row from every other 400 (`ccd/codexbridge/server.py:611`).
- **Why:** Task 8 must rebuild the overflow answer and no other. Finding that answer by reading its message or code
  would tie the server to the table's wording.
- **Cost if wrong:** The attribute is additive. A `BridgeError` built elsewhere without `kind` defaults to `refused`, so
  an overflow built that way would not be rebuilt with its numbers.

### D-6806 — the usage limit's retry-after also reads `resets_in_seconds`

- **Departs from:** The contract's error table (plan :315) and Task 1's test (plan :494-495): `retry-after: <s>` comes
  "from `resets_at` when known".
- **What changed:** `_retry_after_from_reset` reads `resets_at` (epoch seconds) and, when that is absent,
  `resets_in_seconds` (seconds from now). It answers nothing for a time already past
  (`ccd/codexbridge/errors.py:182-191`). The errors suite pins it (`server/test/codex-bridge-errors.test.ts:101`).
- **Why:** The one usage-limit body this repository has seen (spec 2026-09-13, row S1-R10) names `resets_in_seconds`.
  Nothing shows the backend sending `resets_at`, so reading only the plan's field could leave every limit answer without
  a retry-after.
- **Cost if wrong:** If `resets_in_seconds` means anything other than seconds from now, the retry-after is off by the
  difference. `x-should-retry: false` still stops Claude Code retrying, so the only cost is a misleading header.

### D-6807 — `countable_bytes` counts the UTF-8 bytes sent, the developer text once

- **Departs from:** Task 2's count (plan :596-597): the byte length of `json.dumps({"developer", "items", "tools"},
  separators=(",", ":"))`, read together with Step 3 (plan :631), which puts the developer message at `items[0]`.
- **What changed:** The count dumps the items without the leading developer item, with `ensure_ascii=False`, and
  measures the UTF-8 length with `surrogatepass` (`ccd/codexbridge/inbound.py:540-543`, :558; docstring :22-25). The
  `Sys é.` case pins it (`server/test/codex-bridge-inbound.test.ts:549`).
- **Why:** Read literally, the formula counts the system text twice, once as `developer` and once as `items[0]`. Its
  default ASCII escaping also counts a non-ASCII character as 6 bytes rather than the 2-3 it takes on the wire. The
  plan's stated intent is what the bridge sends.
- **Cost if wrong:** D24's ratio is calibrated against this same count, so one consistent definition costs nothing. Only
  a count made the plan's literal way would disagree with it.

### D-6808 — the image-bytes case compares two images, then no image

- **Departs from:** Task 2's case `'images and dropped blocks never count as bytes'` (plan :626-627): two requests equal
  but for a 200 KB image and a signed thinking block, with `countable_bytes` equal and `image_count` 1 and 0.
- **What changed:** The case compares a 200 KB image plus a signed thinking block against a 4-byte image with no
  thinking: equal bytes, `image_count` 1 and 1. It then compares against no image (`image_count` 0), whose difference is
  exactly the emptied `input_image` part (`server/test/codex-bridge-inbound.test.ts:527-547`).
- **Why:** Under the plan's own formula an image's `image_url` is emptied but its part still counts its shape. Equal
  bytes and image counts of 1 and 0 cannot both hold.
- **Cost if wrong:** None in the code; only the test's shape changes. Rows I5 and I14 still turn it red.

### D-6809 — an unknown content block is a plain 400, never dropped

- **Departs from:** Task 2's mapping table (plan :560-587), which names only `document` and server tools among the
  refused inputs and says nothing of any other block type or image source.
- **What changed:** A content block of any other type (user side, assistant side or inside a tool result), and an image
  source that is neither base64 nor url, get a plain 400 naming the bounded type, with `x-should-retry: false`, counted
  `unsupportedField:<type>` as a server tool is (`ccd/codexbridge/inbound.py:130-135`, :204, :228, :378). The case is at
  `server/test/codex-bridge-inbound.test.ts:574`.
- **Why:** Dropping an unknown block silently would lose content the model was meant to see. A retry would send the same
  block again, so the answer says not to retry.
- **Cost if wrong:** A new block type Claude Code starts sending fails the request until it is mapped. The type is named
  in the refusal and in the stats file the first time it appears.

### D-6810 — only the billing block's first line is removed

- **Departs from:** Task 2's system row (plan :564): "a block starting `BILLING_PREFIX` removed".
